The workspace-relative component walk is unchanged. Successful cleanup preserves the original success or failure; cleanup failure still reports the prior error. TempDir provides best-effort Drop cleanup. As documented by tempfile, failed explicit close consumes ownership without an automatic second cleanup attempt; its failure stays visible. No keep/into_path/manual ownership shortcut is used. TLC and Apalache changes only migrate Scratch path access.\n\nParent process identity, descriptor duplication, resource limits, exact-child polling/blocking waits, fixture parent/group identity, fixture subreaper admission and all-owned-child reaping now use rustix. `Running` stores a positive typed Pid. Exact-child waits pass `Some(pid)`; the isolated fixture's any-child reaper uses `wait(empty)`, because `waitpid(None)` only selects the caller's process group. EINTR retry, exit-status conversion/cache and owner-pipe-before-wait Drop ordering are retained. Descriptor duplication still requests minimum10 and CLOEXEC. The optional/infinite or overflowing soft limit saturates at i32::MAX; existing `/dev/fd` enumeration still includes descriptors above a lowered soft limit.\n\n## Scope and remaining raw calls\n\nThe source baseline is `fb25091a0e73d3c25a2569cd811f78ac31d2eb5f`. Similar functionality was extended in the existing process owner; no competing scratch or process abstraction was introduced. The preparation research and three process/capture memories supplied the boundary constraints. All source changes are within process, the explicitly authorized mechanical TLC/Apalache callers and workspace dependency declarations/lock edges. Formatting two unrelated xtask files was incidental to cargo fmt --all; their exact baseline bytes were restored and they are absent from this task's diff. Final formatting checks are scoped to the three owned consumers. No Flow lifecycle/review state or feature map was changed.\n\nRemaining unsafe/raw operations have specific boundary reasons:\n\n- Raw clone/fork and the transfer to child_main preserve the reviewed cancellation/atfork bypass and architecture-specific s390x/SPARC contracts. A general safe fork wrapper is not equivalent.\n- Parent preparation of libc sigaction/sigset structures and SignalMask block/restore preserve the exact kernel signal representation, reserved-signal blocking and eager binding used by the raw child. They cannot be independently replaced with a general wrapper without changing that shared contract.\n- calls::Api::new resolves the audited libc symbols, cancellation-free Darwin entries, errno/TLS and transitive signal/cancellation bindings before fork. Its unsafe ABI work remains necessary to prepare the existing child table.\n- The complete forked supervisor, target and transitive cleanup/error paths remain prepared raw calls: child subreaper, wait/reap loops, signals, descriptor closure, procfs reads, NPROC, exec fallback and _exit. No rustix/tempfile/std/allocator/lock/TLS lookup/unwind/destructor was introduced after fork.\n- restrict_descendants intentionally exercises the same raw NPROC helper used by the production target; converting this fixture alone would weaken its proof. poison_atfork_child intentionally installs a raw pthread_atfork callback and _exit control.\n\n`child-boundary.json` verifies byte identity for the production region from SignalMask through restrict_descendants, and whole-file identity for syscall/calls.rs. Prepared executable fallback and clone argument/result helpers remain in that identical region. The source hashes are in `verified-source-hashes.json`.\n\n## Unsafe inventory\n\nThe same lexical algorithm over every process src/**/*.rs, including all cfg branches and inline tests, counts unsafe blocks (`unsafe\\s*\\{`) and named unsafe function definitions (`unsafe\\s+(?:extern\\s+\"[^\"]+\"\\s+)?fn\\s+[\\w$]+\\s*\\(`). This includes the macro template unsafe method, but excludes function-pointer types and unsafe extern declarations. Matched entries were inspected as actual code, rather than comments/string false positives. This is source accounting, not macro-expanded or runtime/architecture coverage.\n\nBefore:52 blocks+25 definitions=77. After:42 blocks+25 definitions=67. All10 removed blocks were actual parent operations: UID1, try_wait1, Drop wait1, fcntl/ownership2, getrlimit/initialization2, fixture parent identity1, fixture subreaper1, fixture reaping1. syscall.rs falls36→26 blocks with15 definitions unchanged; calls.rs retains16 blocks+10 definitions. No unsafe blocks were grouped, moved, hidden behind new macros, cfg-trimmed or reclassified to lower the count. Safe support/tests remain unsafe-free; workspace forbid and crate-local deny/syscall-only allow are unchanged. Both inventories retain per-file source hashes and line-level sites.\n\n## Dependency and guest accounting\n\nBoth crates were already resolved. to one timed required-backend validation pipeline, focused intermediate checks and frozen milestone bookkeeping during validation.\n\n## Goal & Context\n\nRemove Python from the active Model/framework development toolchain while preserving Model semantics and operational safety under native Rust tool contracts. Shared process ownership belongs in `umpire-backend-process`, separate from TLA+ emission. A host-only `xtask` replaces build helpers, their embedded Python glue and controls. The retained-original Python hash verifier is retired, not ported; the Rust lifecycle comparison stays and no retained artifact changes. Go stays where the host requires it; Bash remains orchestration.\n\nDevelopers use documented native Rust driver contracts, with callers updated together. Keep cheap useful happy-path invocations; no aliases, diagnostic spellings or old encodings are required solely for backward compatibility. The post-fn-237.3 integrated guest is semantic reference evidence, not a byte oracle. This work changes no Model, checker meaning, protocol, IR or Temporal feature behavior.\n\n## Architecture & Data Models\n\n`umpire-backend-process` owns the existing `Limits`, `Refusal`, `Scratch`, `ProcessFailure`, Java admission, process execution and bounded-text contracts. TLA+, TLC and Apalache use it directly where they need those contracts; Apalache acquires no TLC dependency. TLA+ retains binding, emission, Snowcat and typed-value admission. Update callers coherently where the native interface differs; preserve functional typed failure/status mapping and all process safety controls. Old signatures, compatibility reexports and diagnostic wording are not acceptance criteria solely for backward compatibility.\n\nUse an allocation-free process supervisor inside the process crate. Prepare executable resolution, argument/environment bytes, descriptors and bounded buffers before forking. Every path in the forked supervisor and its pre-exec target child is restricted to audited libc calls and stack-only scalar/byte operations; it cannot allocate, format, lock, run Rust destructors, unwind or call general `std` process/filesystem helpers. All child exits use `_exit`. This is the production path for focused Cargo tests and library consumers; no sibling executable, runtime Cargo invocation or warm workspace build is required. A standalone Rust helper is an alternative only if a reviewed build/discovery contract first proves all those use cases.\n\nLinux retains the private supervisor group, target session, subreaper and adopted-descendant cleanup, including escaped process groups. Darwin retains non-root admission and descendant prevention. Other platforms refuse at admission. Signals and owner-liveness EOF preserve the existing outcome mapping. Safe Rust retains capture handles, inode checks, output concatenation, dump admission and scratch cleanup. After the supervisor proof, scratch directories use `tempfile::Builder` and parent-side syscalls use `rustix` safe wrappers; the forked child's async-signal-safe path stays raw libc. Limits keep their existing units and enforcement: Java heap configuration is not an OS memory cap, and polling a regular output file is not a strict kernel output cap.\n\n`xtask` is a native development-profile binary, excluded from guest/wasm dependency closures. Its commands use documented native Rust arguments, JSON schemas, status/error rendering and TOML behavior; retain useful ordinary invocations without emulating Python quirks. It hashes in-process with `sha2` (no `shasum` subprocess), encodes JSON with plain `serde_json` (default BTreeMap-backed `Map`; `preserve_order` off workspace-wide), and handles OS arguments with `std::env::args_os`/`OsString` (no clap), decoding command tokens only where needed. Internal commands replace cache-key calculation, launcher publication and Cargo-artifact selection formerly embedded in the guest shell. Compile xtask before setting guest wrapper/encoded flags; the compiler wrapper directly executes the built native tool, never recursively starts Cargo. Preserve raw compiler argv, previous-wrapper execution, jobserver descriptors and macOS loader environment.\n\n## API Contracts\n\nPreserve `run_process` and cancellable execution's distinction between refusal, child status and retained output. Preserve timeout/cancellation output, first-signal handling, owner EOF, target signal mapping, `125` owner-loss status and `128+signal` outcomes as applicable. Diagnostic text may follow the native interface; callers must still distinguish typed refusals, launch failures and child outcomes.\n\nGuest identities, hash inputs and sidecar records use native Rust schemas and remain deterministic, portable and stable going forward. Invalidate stale caches when encoding changes instead of supporting old formats. Sidecars publish before compilation and disappear on failure. Closure identity retains checksums, non-dev edges across all platforms, build dependencies, edge structure, ambiguity and shared-feature unification behavior.\n\nGuest check mode distinguishes fresh, stale/missing and tool/build/discovery failure, reports useful differences and never publishes the candidate. Ordinary useful status values may remain; old Python formatting/first-offset rendering is not required. Missing prerequisites and probe/metadata/build/staleness failures are explicit failures under the native driver contract, regardless of CI presence. This helper policy change does not change R5 backend default/skip behavior.\n\nRust/Cargo and the documented mise toolchain are prerequisites. Freshness enters through a minimal Bash bootstrap launcher that sets `MISE_AUTO_INSTALL=false` and `MISE_EXEC_AUTO_INSTALL=false` before its first probe. Missing bootstrap tools and bootstrap compilation errors fail clearly without guest build or publication. Bootstrap xtask before guest wrapper/flags are installed; Rust then owns authoritative TOML/JSON parsing, installed-tool metadata, pin consistency, Rust/rustup/Go/protoc/rust-src/wasm readiness and freshness validation. Do not duplicate semantic validators in Bash or retain the old local/CI missing-tool skip matrix. An existing cache must not bypass native validation. Prove the actual cold Make/shell entrypoint, disabled auto-install from the first probe, fail-fast/compiler-failure behavior and never-publish check mode.\n\nThe retained-original hash integrity check is thrown away (owner, 2026-10-24): `verify.py` is removed, not ported, and `manifest.json` (paths and hashes) is deleted. The originals stay in Git as historical evidence; the completed fn-227 checksum artifact under `.flow/artifacts` is history and is not consulted. The compatibility checkpoint keeps its semantic lifecycle comparison against `original/rows.snap.txt` and its mutation control.\n\n## Edge Cases & Constraints\n\nExecution admission requires fn-237.3 done, with its implementation and Flow closure commits present in `umpire` ancestry.
